result3. Command output
# Steel SCC Ladle-Energy Reschedule

A steel plant needs a one-day steelmaking-continuous casting schedule for a
mix of automotive, line-pipe, bearing, electrical, and structural heats. The
plant must coordinate converter furnaces, ladle furnaces, vacuum and CAS
refining, continuous casters, ladles, transfer cranes, oxygen supply, and
time-varying electricity prices.

You are supporting a steel plant's short-term control room. The plant must
publish a steelmaking-continuous casting schedule for one operating day and a
scenario-ready recovery plan for common disruptions. Each heat travels through
converter steelmaking, secondary refining, and continuous casting while sharing
ladles, cranes, oxygen supply, and peak-price electricity periods.

The submitted plan must cover every listed disruption scenario. The equipment
and ladle choices are a published commitment and must remain consistent across
scenarios. Start times may change by scenario to recover from slower converter
work, delayed vacuum refining, or tighter oxygen supply. Converter operations
may use oxygen cutback mode when needed; this saves oxygen at the expense of a
longer converter cycle and an operating penalty.


## Planning Goal

Minimize the risk-weighted operating score across scenarios. The score includes
weighted heat tardiness, excessive waiting that causes temperature loss,
electricity cost during processing, oxygen shortage and venting penalties,
oxygen-cutback penalties, and casting continuity penalties.

## Hard Operating Rules

Every heat must be processed through all stages in its route and must be cast
on its assigned caster as part of its listed cast string. A heat cannot skip a
required route stage or use a machine that is not eligible for the heat's grade
family, width, and process stage.

Stages for a heat must occur in route order. Minimum transfer times between
stages are listed in `config.json`. A heat cannot wait too long between the end
of its final refining stage and the start of casting because temperature and
superheat would fall below the operating target.

Each machine handles at most one heat at a time. When two consecutive heats on
the same machine have different grade families, the setup time in
`transition_rules.csv` must fit between the two operations.

Each heat uses one ladle across its route. The ladle must be compatible with
the heat's required ladle family and grade family, have enough capacity, and
avoid maintenance windows. The same ladle cannot serve overlapping heats,
including the post-cast cleanout time.

Within each cast string, casting must follow the listed cast sequence. The gap
between consecutive heats in the same cast string must stay within the cast's
maximum break allowance. The cast string must use the caster listed in
`casts.csv` and `heats.csv`.

Transfer crane load is checked in time buckets. Refining and casting starts
consume the crane minutes listed in `crane_requirements.csv`; the total crane
minutes in a bucket cannot exceed the bucket capacity in
`oxygen_energy_slots.csv`.

The oxygen and energy bucket table is used for operating score calculations.
Oxygen demand is accumulated from BOF and RH operations, adjusted by scenario
and cutback mode. Demand above the bucket supply receives a shortage penalty;
large unused oxygen capacity receives a smaller venting penalty. Processing
energy is costed against the bucket electricity prices.

Secondary-refining support is also checked in time buckets. LF, CAS, RH, and
casting starts consume alloy-crew and laboratory minutes by grade family. These
shared support teams are limited by `secondary_resource_slots.csv`; a schedule
that overloads those teams is not operationally usable even if the machines
themselves are free.

Ladle thermal condition matters. If a ladle sits cold too long before the BOF
tap for its next heat, it consumes preheat burner minutes shortly before that
heat starts. The preheat burner bucket capacity is limited, and the preheat
minutes add operating cost. Choosing ladles and timings without regard to this
thermal state can make an otherwise plausible schedule fail.

## Data Dictionary

`heats.csv` lists the mandatory heats, cast string, cast order, assigned caster,
grade family, width, weight, route, release time, due time, priority, cooling
sensitivity, and required ladle family.

`casts.csv` lists cast-string timing windows, caster assignment, target gap,
maximum break allowance, and tundish family.

`route_steps.csv` lists the process stages and nominal durations, oxygen use,
and power load for each route.

`machines.csv` lists process equipment, stage, width range, eligible grade
families, and first-ready time.

`transition_rules.csv` gives sequence-dependent setup times by stage and grade
family transition.

`ladles.csv` and `ladle_grade_compatibility.csv` describe ladle availability,
capacity, family, cleanout time, maintenance windows, and grade compatibility.

`scenarios.csv`, `scenario_stage_multipliers.csv`, and
`scenario_heat_delays.csv` describe the disruption cases used for recovery
timing and oxygen calculations.

`oxygen_energy_slots.csv` gives time-bucket oxygen supply, crane capacity, and
electricity prices. The `oxygen_supply_tonnes_OXYGEN_TIGHT` column overrides
the normal oxygen supply in that scenario.

`crane_requirements.csv` gives the crane minutes consumed when a heat starts a
refining or casting stage.

`secondary_resource_slots.csv` and `secondary_resource_requirements.csv` give
the bucket capacities and grade-specific secondary support minutes for alloy
handling, lab sampling, and ladle preheat.

## Required Output

Create these artifacts under `submissions/`:

- `model.md`: your interpretation of the planning problem and the optimization
  or heuristic method you used.
- `solve.py`: the script used to create the submitted schedule.
- `solution.csv`: the operation schedule.
- `solve_log.md`: commands run, method status, objective or score if available,
  and feasibility checks.

The `solution.csv` file must have exactly these columns:

```csv
row_type,scenario_id,heat_id,stage,machine_id,start_min,ladle_id,cast_id,caster_id,oxygen_mode,notes
```

Use one `operation` row for every required heat-stage pair in every scenario.
The `stage` values come from the heat's route in `route_steps.csv`; every heat
has exactly one casting stage. Use `start_min` as an integer minute from the
start of the planning horizon. Use the same `machine_id` and `ladle_id` for a
given heat-stage or heat across all scenarios. The `oxygen_mode` field is
`normal` or `cutback`; cutback may only be used on BOF operations and represents
a slower, oxygen-saving converter mode.

---
name: optimization-modeling-core
description: Use for natural-language-to-optimization tasks that require translating a business problem into a complete mathematical model before writing solver code.
metadata:
  short-description: Turn business text into a full optimization model
---

# Optimization Modeling Core

## Use when

- The user gives a word problem, business process, paper scenario, or benchmark prompt and expects a formal optimization model.
- Solver code exists but the issue appears to be a missing or incorrect formulation.
- The task asks for a complete model, not only a heuristic, simulation, or data summary.

## Do not use when

- The task is purely about software plumbing, visualization, data cleaning, or report writing.
- The problem is already fully formulated and the user only asks for code translation into a specific solver; use the solver-specific skill as the primary guide.
- The request is exploratory and the user explicitly wants brainstorming without implementation.

## Model-first workflow

1. Restate the business decision in plain language.
2. Identify sets, indices, units, and time granularity.
3. Extract parameters from data files and confirm units.
4. Define decision variables with domains and business meaning.
5. Write the objective with every cost, reward, penalty, and service term.
6. Write constraints grouped by operational rule.
7. Check edge cases: missing eligibility, impossible demand, fixed commitments, and terminal-state requirements.
8. Only then implement code.

## Ambiguity handling

- If the brief is ambiguous but data and examples imply a convention, state the assumption and continue.
- If ambiguity changes feasibility, objective direction, or deliverable schema, ask before coding or implement the least risky interpretation with a clear note.
- If units conflict across files, normalize units explicitly before modeling.
- If a hard requirement conflicts with data, diagnose the conflict before adding slack variables.

## Formulation checklist

- Every row in an input table should either become a parameter, an eligibility rule, or be explicitly irrelevant.
- Every decision variable should appear in at least one binding constraint and usually in the objective.
- Demand balance should use the correct equality or inequality direction; do not weaken equality to `>=` unless surplus is genuinely allowed.
- Capacity constraints should include activation/open/setup binaries when the business says inactive resources cannot produce, ship, or serve.
- Shortage, backlog, overtime, outsourcing, and postponement variables need explicit cost and bounds.
- Multi-period models need state transition equations and initial/terminal states.
- If the user asks for explanation, describe the modeling logic in business terms after solving.

## Constraint boundary rules

- Hard constraints represent physical impossibility, policy mandates, legal requirements, or explicit benchmark feasibility rules.
- Soft constraints need penalty variables or objective terms; do not silently turn hard constraints into penalties.
- Service levels should specify numerator, denominator, eligible population, and time window.
- Fairness constraints should define the comparison group and whether unavailable resources count.
- Logical implications need both directions only when the business rule says equivalence, not merely implication.

## Objective boundary rules

- Confirm whether the problem minimizes cost, maximizes profit, maximizes service, or uses lexicographic priorities.
- Do not mix penalties with primary economics unless their scale is intentional.
- Include fixed costs only once per activated entity.
- Include revenue only for fulfilled demand or accepted jobs, not for unmet or canceled work.
- Keep reporting metrics separate from optimized objective terms unless the prompt explicitly optimizes them.

## Implementation expectations

- Use structured parsing for CSV/JSON instead of manual string manipulation.
- Keep solver code, solution export, and validation code clearly separated.
- For benchmark or task environments, read the provided brief before coding and follow the expected output schema exactly.
- When comparing two answers, diagnose concrete formulation differences and objective consequences, not only feasibility.
- Write exported solutions at the precision expected by the checker; avoid unnecessary rounding.
- Preserve the input schema and output schema unless the user asks to redesign the benchmark.

## Verification boundary

- Verify feasibility independently from solver status when an evaluator or checker exists.
- Recompute objective from exported artifacts, not only from in-memory solver expressions.
- For benchmark tasks, create at least one negative check for each newly added rule when practical.
- For paper or real-world tasks, separate what is directly supported by the source from synthetic or assumed data.

## Red flags

- Objective terms that are counted twice or charged at the wrong marginal rate.
- Binary variables not linked tightly enough to continuous activity.
- Time periods shifted by one index.
- Average or fairness constraints that ignore the denominator or eligibility set.
- Public prompt files that reveal solver-ready variables or formulas when the benchmark is meant to test modeling translation.
- A feasible solution that exploits missing eligibility, free disposal, negative cycles, or unbounded production.
- A model that reports optimality after a time limit, infeasible status, or missing incumbent.

/bin/bash -lc "python - <<'PY'
import csv, json
from collections import Counter,defaultdict
base='/app'
# basic metadata
with open(base+'/submissions/solution.csv', newline='') as f:
    sol=list(csv.DictReader(f))
with open(base+'/data/heats.csv', newline='') as f:
    heats=list(csv.DictReader(f))
with open(base+'/data/route_steps.csv', newline='') as f:
    route_steps=list(csv.DictReader(f))
with open(base+'/data/scenarios.csv', newline='') as f:
    scenarios=list(csv.DictReader(f))
print('solution rows', len(sol))
print('scenarios', [r['scenario_id'] for r in scenarios])
print('heats', len(heats))
# route lengths
by_route=defaultdict(list)
for r in route_steps:
    by_route[r['route_id']].append(r)
for k in by_route:
    by_route[k].sort(key=lambda r:int(r['step_no']))
print('routes', {k:[r['stage'] for r in v] for k,v in by_route.items()})
print('route counts', Counter(h['route_id'] for h in heats))
# rows per scenario and stage counts
print('rows/scenario', Counter(r['scenario_id'] for r in sol))
print('stages/scenario', {s: Counter(r['stage'] for r in sol if r['scenario_id']==s) for s in Counter(r['scenario_id'] for r in sol)})
# unique row_types
print('row_types', Counter(r['row_type'] for r in sol))
# machine/ladle consistency counts
heat_stage_ml=defaultdict(set)
heat_ladle=defaultdict(set)
for r in sol:
    heat_stage_ml[(r['heat_id'],r['stage'])].add(r['machine_id'])
    heat_ladle[r['heat_id']].add(r['ladle_id'])
print('inconsistent machines', sum(1 for v in heat_stage_ml.values() if len(v)>1))
print('inconsistent ladles', sum(1 for v in heat_ladle.values() if len(v)>1))
PY"

result3. Command output
---
name: optimization-modeling-core
description: Use for natural-language-to-optimization tasks that require translating a business problem into a complete mathematical model before writing solver code.
metadata:
  short-description: Turn business text into a full optimization model
---

# Optimization Modeling Core

## Use when

- The user gives a word problem, business process, paper scenario, or benchmark prompt and expects a formal optimization model.
- Solver code exists but the issue appears to be a missing or incorrect formulation.
- The task asks for a complete model, not only a heuristic, simulation, or data summary.

## Do not use when

- The task is purely about software plumbing, visualization, data cleaning, or report writing.
- The problem is already fully formulated and the user only asks for code translation into a specific solver; use the solver-specific skill as the primary guide.
- The request is exploratory and the user explicitly wants brainstorming without implementation.

## Model-first workflow

1. Restate the business decision in plain language.
2. Identify sets, indices, units, and time granularity.
3. Extract parameters from data files and confirm units.
4. Define decision variables with domains and business meaning.
5. Write the objective with every cost, reward, penalty, and service term.
6. Write constraints grouped by operational rule.
7. Check edge cases: missing eligibility, impossible demand, fixed commitments, and terminal-state requirements.
8. Only then implement code.

## Ambiguity handling

- If the brief is ambiguous but data and examples imply a convention, state the assumption and continue.
- If ambiguity changes feasibility, objective direction, or deliverable schema, ask before coding or implement the least risky interpretation with a clear note.
- If units conflict across files, normalize units explicitly before modeling.
- If a hard requirement conflicts with data, diagnose the conflict before adding slack variables.

## Formulation checklist

- Every row in an input table should either become a parameter, an eligibility rule, or be explicitly irrelevant.
- Every decision variable should appear in at least one binding constraint and usually in the objective.
- Demand balance should use the correct equality or inequality direction; do not weaken equality to `>=` unless surplus is genuinely allowed.
- Capacity constraints should include activation/open/setup binaries when the business says inactive resources cannot produce, ship, or serve.
- Shortage, backlog, overtime, outsourcing, and postponement variables need explicit cost and bounds.
- Multi-period models need state transition equations and initial/terminal states.
- If the user asks for explanation, describe the modeling logic in business terms after solving.

## Constraint boundary rules

- Hard constraints represent physical impossibility, policy mandates, legal requirements, or explicit benchmark feasibility rules.
- Soft constraints need penalty variables or objective terms; do not silently turn hard constraints into penalties.
- Service levels should specify numerator, denominator, eligible population, and time window.
- Fairness constraints should define the comparison group and whether unavailable resources count.
- Logical implications need both directions only when the business rule says equivalence, not merely implication.

## Objective boundary rules

- Confirm whether the problem minimizes cost, maximizes profit, maximizes service, or uses lexicographic priorities.
- Do not mix penalties with primary economics unless their scale is intentional.
- Include fixed costs only once per activated entity.
- Include revenue only for fulfilled demand or accepted jobs, not for unmet or canceled work.
- Keep reporting metrics separate from optimized objective terms unless the prompt explicitly optimizes them.

## Implementation expectations

- Use structured parsing for CSV/JSON instead of manual string manipulation.
- Keep solver code, solution export, and validation code clearly separated.
- For benchmark or task environments, read the provided brief before coding and follow the expected output schema exactly.
- When comparing two answers, diagnose concrete formulation differences and objective consequences, not only feasibility.
- Write exported solutions at the precision expected by the checker; avoid unnecessary rounding.
- Preserve the input schema and output schema unless the user asks to redesign the benchmark.

## Verification boundary

- Verify feasibility independently from solver status when an evaluator or checker exists.
- Recompute objective from exported artifacts, not only from in-memory solver expressions.
- For benchmark tasks, create at least one negative check for each newly added rule when practical.
- For paper or real-world tasks, separate what is directly supported by the source from synthetic or assumed data.

## Red flags

- Objective terms that are counted twice or charged at the wrong marginal rate.
- Binary variables not linked tightly enough to continuous activity.
- Time periods shifted by one index.
- Average or fairness constraints that ignore the denominator or eligibility set.
- Public prompt files that reveal solver-ready variables or formulas when the benchmark is meant to test modeling translation.
- A feasible solution that exploits missing eligibility, free disposal, negative cycles, or unbounded production.
- A model that reports optimality after a time limit, infeasible status, or missing incumbent.

---
name: or-pyscipopt
description: Use when building, running, validating, or debugging Python optimization models that use PySCIPOpt and SCIP.
metadata:
  short-description: Build and run PySCIPOpt models
---

# OR PySCIPOpt

## Use when

- The task asks for a Python optimization model using SCIP or `pyscipopt`.
- Existing solver code imports `pyscipopt`, sets SCIP parameters, writes `.lp`/`.mps`, or calls a SCIP-based checker.
- The user asks to run, debug, speed up, validate, or explain a SCIP-based solve.

## Do not use when

- The task only needs a mathematical formulation and no solver-specific code.
- The project clearly uses a different solver stack, such as Pyomo with CBC/HiGHS, OR-Tools CP-SAT, PuLP, CPLEX, or cvxpy, unless the user asks to port it to PySCIPOpt.
- The model is nonlinear or stochastic in a way that SCIP cannot handle directly; first decide whether it can be linearized or approximated.

## Environment rule

Use the Python environment specified by the user or the project. In this ORClaw workspace, the default solver environment is the `or` conda environment with PySCIPOpt available:

```bash
conda run -n or python <script.py>
```

If `pyscipopt` is missing, report the missing dependency, identify the command that failed, and try the project-specified environment before changing solver stacks.

## Workflow

1. Read the task brief and input files before coding.
2. Write the full mathematical model first: sets, parameters, decision variables, objective, and constraints.
3. Implement the model with explicit tuple keys and named constraints.
4. Solve with the selected Python command.
5. Export the solution using the exact schema required by the task.
6. Validate with the task checker or an independent recomputation of feasibility and objective.
7. If the solution is not accepted, diagnose whether the issue is formulation, data parsing, export formatting, tolerance, or evaluator mismatch before changing the objective or constraints.

## PySCIPOpt patterns

- Use `from pyscipopt import Model, quicksum`.
- Create sparse variable dictionaries explicitly, for example:

```python
x = {(i, j, t): model.addVar(vtype="B", name=f"x[{i},{j},{t}]") for i, j, t in keys}
```

- Use `quicksum(...)` for large linear expressions.
- Use `model.addCons(expr <= rhs, name="capacity[k,t]")` for constraints.
- Use `model.setObjective(expr, "minimize")` or `model.setObjective(expr, "maximize")`.
- Set reproducibility and limits with SCIP parameters:

```python
model.setParam("limits/gap", 0.0005)
model.setParam("limits/time", time_limit)
model.setParam("display/verblevel", 0)
```

- After `model.optimize()`, inspect `model.getStatus()` and only read values if a solution exists.
- Read variable values with `model.getVal(x[key])`, not `x[key].X`.
- Read objective and bound with `model.getObjVal()` and `model.getDualbound()`.
- Read relative gap with `model.getGap()` when available.

## Modeling hygiene

- Calibrate every big-M from data. If a tight M is not obvious, derive a bound and document it in a short comment.
- Keep feasibility checks, objective recomputation, and reference-optimality comparison separate.
- Do not round exported decision values unless the schema explicitly requires it.
- For benchmark work, treat the evaluator or checker as the scoring authority and the reference solver as a validation baseline.
- Keep solver logs or a concise solve summary when the user needs auditability.
- Avoid changing data to make a model feasible unless the task is explicitly to design or repair an instance.

## Debugging boundaries

- If a model is infeasible, inspect hard lower bounds, conservation equations, and mutually exclusive binary logic before relaxing constraints.
- If objective values differ, recompute each objective component from exported decisions.
- If runtime is high, first remove dominated variables, tighten big-M values, add eligibility filters, and check whether binaries are really necessary.
- If SCIP returns an incumbent with a gap, say so clearly; do not call it exact optimality unless the status and gap support that claim.
- If an evaluator rejects a solution that the model considers feasible, replay the rejected rule directly from the exported file before changing solver code.

## Useful commands

```bash
python -c "import pyscipopt; print(pyscipopt.__version__)"
conda run -n or python -c "import pyscipopt; print(pyscipopt.__version__)"
<python-command> <solver-script.py>
<python-command> <checker-script.py> --solution <solution-file> --env-dir <task-dir>
```

# Steel SCC Ladle-Energy Reschedule

A steel plant needs a one-day steelmaking-continuous casting schedule for a
mix of automotive, line-pipe, bearing, electrical, and structural heats. The
plant must coordinate converter furnaces, ladle furnaces, vacuum and CAS
refining, continuous casters, ladles, transfer cranes, oxygen supply, and
time-varying electricity prices.

You are supporting a steel plant's short-term control room. The plant must
publish a steelmaking-continuous casting schedule for one operating day and a
scenario-ready recovery plan for common disruptions. Each heat travels through
converter steelmaking, secondary refining, and continuous casting while sharing
ladles, cranes, oxygen supply, and peak-price electricity periods.

The submitted plan must cover every listed disruption scenario. The equipment
and ladle choices are a published commitment and must remain consistent across
scenarios. Start times may change by scenario to recover from slower converter
work, delayed vacuum refining, or tighter oxygen supply. Converter operations
may use oxygen cutback mode when needed; this saves oxygen at the expense of a
longer converter cycle and an operating penalty.


## Planning Goal

Minimize the risk-weighted operating score across scenarios. The score includes
weighted heat tardiness, excessive waiting that causes temperature loss,
electricity cost during processing, oxygen shortage and venting penalties,
oxygen-cutback penalties, and casting continuity penalties.

## Hard Operating Rules

Every heat must be processed through all stages in its route and must be cast
on its assigned caster as part of its listed cast string. A heat cannot skip a
required route stage or use a machine that is not eligible for the heat's grade
family, width, and process stage.

Stages for a heat must occur in route order. Minimum transfer times between
stages are listed in `config.json`. A heat cannot wait too long between the end
of its final refining stage and the start of casting because temperature and
superheat would fall below the operating target.

Each machine handles at most one heat at a time. When two consecutive heats on
the same machine have different grade families, the setup time in
`transition_rules.csv` must fit between the two operations.

Each heat uses one ladle across its route. The ladle must be compatible with
the heat's required ladle family and grade family, have enough capacity, and
avoid maintenance windows. The same ladle cannot serve overlapping heats,
including the post-cast cleanout time.

Within each cast string, casting must follow the listed cast sequence. The gap
between consecutive heats in the same cast string must stay within the cast's
maximum break allowance. The cast string must use the caster listed in
`casts.csv` and `heats.csv`.

Transfer crane load is checked in time buckets. Refining and casting starts
consume the crane minutes listed in `crane_requirements.csv`; the total crane
minutes in a bucket cannot exceed the bucket capacity in
`oxygen_energy_slots.csv`.

The oxygen and energy bucket table is used for operating score calculations.
Oxygen demand is accumulated from BOF and RH operations, adjusted by scenario
and cutback mode. Demand above the bucket supply receives a shortage penalty;
large unused oxygen capacity receives a smaller venting penalty. Processing
energy is costed against the bucket electricity prices.

Secondary-refining support is also checked in time buckets. LF, CAS, RH, and
casting starts consume alloy-crew and laboratory minutes by grade family. These
shared support teams are limited by `secondary_resource_slots.csv`; a schedule
that overloads those teams is not operationally usable even if the machines
themselves are free.

Ladle thermal condition matters. If a ladle sits cold too long before the BOF
tap for its next heat, it consumes preheat burner minutes shortly before that
heat starts. The preheat burner bucket capacity is limited, and the preheat
minutes add operating cost. Choosing ladles and timings without regard to this
thermal state can make an otherwise plausible schedule fail.

## Data Dictionary

`heats.csv` lists the mandatory heats, cast string, cast order, assigned caster,
grade family, width, weight, route, release time, due time, priority, cooling
sensitivity, and required ladle family.

`casts.csv` lists cast-string timing windows, caster assignment, target gap,
maximum break allowance, and tundish family.

`route_steps.csv` lists the process stages and nominal durations, oxygen use,
and power load for each route.

`machines.csv` lists process equipment, stage, width range, eligible grade
families, and first-ready time.

`transition_rules.csv` gives sequence-dependent setup times by stage and grade
family transition.

`ladles.csv` and `ladle_grade_compatibility.csv` describe ladle availability,
capacity, family, cleanout time, maintenance windows, and grade compatibility.

`scenarios.csv`, `scenario_stage_multipliers.csv`, and
`scenario_heat_delays.csv` describe the disruption cases used for recovery
timing and oxygen calculations.

`oxygen_energy_slots.csv` gives time-bucket oxygen supply, crane capacity, and
electricity prices. The `oxygen_supply_tonnes_OXYGEN_TIGHT` column overrides
the normal oxygen supply in that scenario.

`crane_requirements.csv` gives the crane minutes consumed when a heat starts a
refining or casting stage.

`secondary_resource_slots.csv` and `secondary_resource_requirements.csv` give
the bucket capacities and grade-specific secondary support minutes for alloy
handling, lab sampling, and ladle preheat.

## Required Output

Create these artifacts under `submissions/`:

- `model.md`: your interpretation of the planning problem and the optimization
  or heuristic method you used.
- `solve.py`: the script used to create the submitted schedule.
- `solution.csv`: the operation schedule.
- `solve_log.md`: commands run, method status, objective or score if available,
  and feasibility checks.

The `solution.csv` file must have exactly these columns:

```csv
row_type,scenario_id,heat_id,stage,machine_id,start_min,ladle_id,cast_id,caster_id,oxygen_mode,notes
```

Use one `operation` row for every required heat-stage pair in every scenario.
The `stage` values come from the heat's route in `route_steps.csv`; every heat
has exactly one casting stage. Use `start_min` as an integer minute from the
start of the planning horizon. Use the same `machine_id` and `ladle_id` for a
given heat-stage or heat across all scenarios. The `oxygen_mode` field is
`normal` or `cutback`; cutback may only be used on BOF operations and represents
a slower, oxygen-saving converter mode.

result66. Command output
---
name: advanced-mip-patterns
description: Use when a task involves fixed charges, logical implications, min/max choices, sequencing, no-overlap, piecewise costs, routing-state logic, or other advanced MIP modeling patterns.
metadata:
  short-description: Advanced MIP formulation patterns
---

# Advanced MIP Patterns

## Use when

- The formulation needs logical implications, fixed charges, activation decisions, ordering, no-overlap, disjunctions, min/max, absolute values, tiers, route state, or resource calendars.
- A simple LP/MIP is producing unrealistic behavior because decisions are not linked tightly enough.
- The model is slow or infeasible and the likely cause is weak big-M logic, excessive pairwise variables, or missing state transitions.

## Do not use when

- A continuous LP is sufficient and adding binaries would not represent a real decision.
- The task is better handled by a specialized CP-SAT, constraint programming, nonlinear, or simulation method and the user did not require MIP.
- The user only needs a high-level explanation; do not introduce advanced constructs unless they clarify the actual model.

## General boundary rules

- Add binaries only for real yes/no choices, disjunctions, or nonconvex structure.
- Prefer the tightest formulation that matches the business rule; avoid decorative binaries.
- Use solver-native constructs when they improve clarity and are supported by the target environment.
- If a construct makes the model much slower, compare it with a simpler valid alternative before keeping it.

## Fixed charge and activation

- Use a binary `y` for whether an activity/resource is active.
- Link activity with a tight bound: `x <= U * y`.
- Add lower activation only when the business requires minimum use: `x >= L * y`.
- Charge fixed cost once through `fixed_cost * y`, not per unit.
- Do not force `y = 1` whenever `x = 0` unless the activity is mandatory.
- If there are startup/shutdown costs over time, separate active state from transition state.

## Logical rules

- Prefer solver-native indicator constraints for simple if-then rules when they keep the model clearer.
- If using big-M, derive M from data bounds and keep one M per rule family when possible.
- For either-or choices, use binaries whose sum is exactly one or at most one, matching the business rule.
- Distinguish implication (`A -> B`) from equivalence (`A <-> B`); the reverse direction often needs a separate constraint.
- For conditional capacity, link both activity and resource use to the condition.

## Min, max, absolute value

- For `z = max(a, b)`, add `z >= a`, `z >= b`, and objective pressure if minimizing z. Use binaries only when equality is needed without objective pressure.
- For absolute deviation `d = |x - target|`, add `d >= x - target` and `d >= target - x`.
- For service-level penalties, separate hard feasibility from soft penalty variables.
- For `min`, either transform with signs or add binaries when equality is not enforced by the objective.
- For ratio constraints, cross-multiply only when denominators are constant or safely bounded away from zero.

## Sequencing and no-overlap

- For jobs sharing a resource, use order binary `before[i,j]`.
- Typical pattern:
  - `start[j] >= end[i] - M * (1 - before[i,j])`
  - `start[i] >= end[j] - M * before[i,j]`
- Compute M from the scheduling horizon, not from an arbitrary large number.
- Include setup, cleanup, travel, rest, or turnover time in the correct side of the ordering constraint.
- Create pairwise order variables only for pairs that can share a resource or conflict.
- If time is discrete and the horizon is small, a time-indexed model may be cleaner than pairwise disjunctions.

## Piecewise and tiers

- Use solver-native piecewise-linear features for convex piecewise costs when suitable.
- For nonconvex tiers, use explicit segment variables and segment activation binaries.
- Ensure tier quantities sum to the original activity and each tier has its own upper bound.
- Distinguish incremental tiers from all-units tiers; their formulations and costs differ.
- Check whether discount tiers create nonconvex incentives that require binary selection.

## State and inventory logic

- State variables need initial conditions, transition equations, and terminal requirements if the business specifies them.
- Route or schedule state should be advanced in event order, not checked only row by row.
- Recovery, restock, cleaning, cooldown, maintenance, and rest rules often require cumulative state or last-event logic.
- If state can reset, model the reset trigger and its resource/time cost.

## Debugging advanced models

- First solve a relaxed or smaller instance and inspect whether the intended binaries activate.
- Add named constraints so IIS output points to business rules.
- If the reference solution is infeasible, replay the relevant state transitions manually before changing the evaluator.
- If the LP relaxation is very weak, inspect big-M values, missing lower/upper links, and symmetry.
- If the model is unexpectedly infeasible, test each advanced rule family on a tiny instance with a known feasible solution.

import csv, json, math
from collections import defaultdict
base='/app/data'
with open(base+'/config.json') as f: cfg=json.load(f)
with open(base+'/heats.csv') as f: heats={r['heat_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(f)}
with open(base+'/casts.csv') as f: casts={r['cast_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(f)}
with open(base+'/route_steps.csv') as f: rs=list(csv.DictReader(f))
with open(base+'/machines.csv') as f: machines=list(csv.DictReader(f))
with open(base+'/transition_rules.csv') as f: tr=list(csv.DictReader(f))
route=defaultdict(list)
for r in rs: route[r['route_id']].append(r)
for k in route: route[k].sort(key=lambda x:int(x['step_no']))
trans={(r['stage'],r['from_family'],r['to_family']):int(r['setup_min']) for r in tr}
stage_m=defaultdict(list)
for m in machines: stage_m[m['stage']].append(m)
cast_heats=defaultdict(list)
for h in heats.values(): cast_heats[h['cast_id']].append(h['heat_id'])
for cid in cast_heats: cast_heats[cid].sort(key=lambda hid:int(heats[hid]['cast_sequence']))
# cast order fixed by target_start
caster_casts=defaultdict(list)
for cid,c in casts.items(): caster_casts[c['caster_id']].append(cid)
for caster in caster_casts: caster_casts[caster].sort(key=lambda cid:int(casts[cid]['target_start_min']))
# base CC starts at targets sequential with setup and target gaps
cc_start={}
cc_end={}
for caster,clist in caster_casts.items():
    t=max(int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m['CC'] if m['machine_id']==caster)),0)
    prev_last=None
    prev_family=None
    for cid in clist:
        fam=casts[cid]['primary_family']
        target=int(casts[cid]['target_start_min'])
        if prev_last is not None:
            t=max(t, prev_last + trans[('CC', prev_family, fam)])
        t=max(t,target)
        cur=t
        for hid in cast_heats[cid]:
            dur=float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='CC'))
            cc_start[hid]=cur
            cc_end[hid]=cur+dur
            cur=cc_end[hid]+int(casts[cid]['target_gap_min'])
        prev_last=cc_end[cast_heats[cid][-1]]
        prev_family=fam
        t=prev_last
print('last cc end', max(cc_end.values()))
# helper to get eligible machines
eligible={}
for hid,h in heats.items():
  for step in route[h['route_id']]:
    st=step['stage']
    elig=[]
    for m in stage_m[st]:
      fams=set(m['eligible_families'].split('|'))
      if h['grade_family'] in fams and int(m['min_width_mm'])<=int(h['width_mm'])<=int(m['max_width_mm']):
        elig.append(m['machine_id'])
    if st=='CC': elig=[h['caster_id']]
    eligible[(hid,st)] = elig
# backward stage scheduling
assign={}
starts={('BASE',hid,'CC'):cc_start[hid] for hid in heats}
ends={('BASE',hid,'CC'):cc_end[hid] for hid in heats}
# final stage jobs by stage
# generic scheduler with machine choice

def sched_stage(stage, jobs, deadlines):
    # deadlines are latest start times for stage jobs
    # process descending deadlines, place latest feasible on a machine
    mach_head={m['machine_id']:float('inf') for m in stage_m[stage]}
    mach_nextfam={m['machine_id']:None for m in stage_m[stage]}
    out={}
    jobs_sorted=sorted(jobs, key=lambda hid:deadlines[hid], reverse=True)
    for hid in jobs_sorted:
        fam=heats[hid]['grade_family']
        dur=float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']==stage))
        best=None
        for mid in eligible[(hid,stage)]:
            head=mach_head[mid]
            nextfam=mach_nextfam[mid]
            setup=0 if nextfam is None else trans[(stage,fam,nextfam)]
            end=min(deadlines[hid]+dur, head-setup)
            start=end-dur
            ready=int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m[stage] if m['machine_id']==mid))
            if stage=='BOF':
                rel=int(heats[hid]['release_min'])
                start_min=max(ready,rel)
            else:
                start_min=ready
            feasible=start>=start_min-1e-9
            cand=(start, mid, end)
            if feasible and (best is None or cand[0]>best[0]):
                best=cand
        if best is None:
            # choose least bad
            bad=[]
            for mid in eligible[(hid,stage)]:
                head=mach_head[mid]
                nextfam=mach_nextfam[mid]
                setup=0 if nextfam is None else trans[(stage,fam,nextfam)]
                end=min(deadlines[hid]+dur, head-setup)
                start=end-dur
                ready=int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m[stage] if m['machine_id']==mid))
                if stage=='BOF':
                    start_min=max(ready,int(heats[hid]['release_min']))
                else:
                    start_min=ready
                bad.append((start-start_min,start,mid,end,start_min))
            bad.sort(reverse=True)
            print('infeas',stage,hid,'best slack',bad[0])
            best=(bad[0][1],bad[0][2],bad[0][3])
        start,mid,end=best
        out[hid]=(mid,start,end)
        mach_head[mid]=start
        mach_nextfam[mid]=fam
    return out
# schedule RH to CC
rh_jobs=[hid for hid,h in heats.items() if any(s['stage']=='RH' for s in route[h['route_id']])]
rh_dead={hid:cc_start[hid]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['RH:CC']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='RH')) for hid in rh_jobs}
rh=sched_stage('RH',rh_jobs,rh_dead)
print('RH machine usage', {m:sum(1 for hid,v in rh.items() if v[0]==m) for m in [x['machine_id'] for x in stage_m['RH']]})
lf_jobs=[hid for hid,h in heats.items() if any(s['stage']=='LF' for s in route[h['route_id']])]
lf_dead={}
for hid in lf_jobs:
    if any(s['stage']=='RH' for s in route[heats[hid]['route_id']]):
        lf_dead[hid]=rh[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:RH']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='LF'))
    else:
        lf_dead[hid]=cc_start[hid]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:CC']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='LF'))
lf=sched_stage('LF',lf_jobs,lf_dead)
print('LF machine usage', {m:sum(1 for hid,v in lf.items() if v[0]==m) for m in [x['machine_id'] for x in stage_m['LF']]})
cas_jobs=[hid for hid,h in heats.items() if any(s['stage']=='CAS' for s in route[h['route_id']])]
cas_dead={hid:lf[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['CAS:LF']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='CAS')) for hid in cas_jobs}
cas=sched_stage('CAS',cas_jobs,cas_dead)
print('CAS usage', len(cas))
bof_jobs=list(heats)
bof_dead={}
for hid in bof_jobs:
    if any(s['stage']=='CAS' for s in route[heats[hid]['route_id']]):
        bof_dead[hid]=cas[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['BOF:CAS']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='BOF'))
    else:
        bof_dead[hid]=lf[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['BOF:LF']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='BOF'))
# Note RH_DEGAS route BOF->LF not BOF->RH
bof=sched_stage('BOF',bof_jobs,bof_dead)
print('BOF machine usage', {m:sum(1 for hid,v in bof.items() if v[0]==m) for m in [x['machine_id'] for x in stage_m['BOF']]})
# print earliest bof
print('min bof start', min(v[1] for v in bof.values()))
# check final waits
mx=0
for hid,h in heats.items():
    if any(s['stage']=='RH' for s in route[h['route_id']]):
        fin_end=rh[hid][2]
        mintr=cfg['min_transfer_min']['RH:CC']
    else:
        fin_end=lf[hid][2]
        mintr=cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:CC']
    wait=cc_start[hid]-fin_end
    mx=max(mx,wait)
print('max final wait',mx)

    prev_last=None; prev_family=None
    for cid in clist:
        fam=casts[cid]['primary_family']; target=int(casts[cid]['target_start_min'])
        if prev_last is not None: t=max(t, prev_last + trans[('CC', prev_family, fam)])
        t=max(t,target); cur=t
        for hid in cast_heats[cid]:
            dur=float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='CC'))
            cc_start[hid]=cur; cc_end[hid]=cur+dur; cur=cc_end[hid]+int(casts[cid]['target_gap_min'])
        prev_last=cc_end[cast_heats[cid][-1]]; prev_family=fam; t=prev_last
eligible={}
for hid,h in heats.items():
  for step in route[h['route_id']]:
    st=step['stage']; elig=[]
    for m in stage_m[st]:
      fams=set(m['eligible_families'].split('|'))
      if h['grade_family'] in fams and int(m['min_width_mm'])<=int(h['width_mm'])<=int(m['max_width_mm']): elig.append(m['machine_id'])
    if st=='CC': elig=[h['caster_id']]
    eligible[(hid,st)] = elig

def sched_stage(stage, jobs, deadlines):
    mach_head={m['machine_id']:float('inf') for m in stage_m[stage]}
    mach_nextfam={m['machine_id']:None for m in stage_m[stage]}
    out={}
    jobs_sorted=sorted(jobs, key=lambda hid:deadlines[hid], reverse=True)
    for hid in jobs_sorted:
        fam=heats[hid]['grade_family']
        dur=float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']==stage))
        best=None
        for mid in eligible[(hid,stage)]:
            head=mach_head[mid]; nextfam=mach_nextfam[mid]
            setup=0 if nextfam is None else trans[(stage,fam,nextfam)]
            end=min(deadlines[hid]+dur, head-setup); start=end-dur
            ready=int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m[stage] if m['machine_id']==mid))
            if stage=='BOF': start_min=max(ready,int(heats[hid]['release_min']))
            else: start_min=ready
            if start>=start_min-1e-9 and (best is None or start>best[1]): best=(mid,start,end)
        if best is None:
            raise RuntimeError(f'no feasible {stage} for {hid}')
        mid,start,end=best; out[hid]=(mid,start,end); mach_head[mid]=start; mach_nextfam[mid]=fam
    return out
rh_jobs=[hid for hid,h in heats.items() if any(s['stage']=='RH' for s in route[h['route_id']])]
rh_dead={hid:cc_start[hid]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['RH:CC']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='RH')) for hid in rh_jobs}
rh=sched_stage('RH',rh_jobs,rh_dead)
lf_jobs=[hid for hid,h in heats.items() if any(s['stage']=='LF' for s in route[h['route_id']])]
lf_dead={}
for hid in lf_jobs:
    if hid in rh: lf_dead[hid]=rh[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:RH']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='LF'))
    else: lf_dead[hid]=cc_start[hid]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:CC']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='LF'))
lf=sched_stage('LF',lf_jobs,lf_dead)
cas_jobs=[hid for hid,h in heats.items() if any(s['stage']=='CAS' for s in route[h['route_id']])]
cas_dead={hid:lf[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['CAS:LF']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='CAS')) for hid in cas_jobs}
cas=sched_stage('CAS',cas_jobs,cas_dead)
bof_jobs=list(heats)
bof_dead={}
for hid in bof_jobs:
    if hid in cas: bof_dead[hid]=cas[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['BOF:CAS']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='BOF'))
    else: bof_dead[hid]=lf[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['BOF:LF']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='BOF'))
bof=sched_stage('BOF',bof_jobs,bof_dead)
# greedy ladles
usage=[]
for hid in heats:
    usage.append((bof[hid][1], cc_end[hid]+int(cfg['ladle_cleanout_min']), hid))
usage.sort()
ladle_intervals=defaultdict(list)
assign={}
for start,end,hid in usage:
    h=heats[hid]
    candidates=[]
    for lid,l in ladles.items():
        if l['ladle_family']"'!=h['"'ladle_family']: continue
        if compat[(l['ladle_family'], h['grade_family'])] "'!= 1: continue
        if float(l['"'capacity_tonnes']) + 1e-9 < float(h['weight_tonnes']): continue
        ready=float(l['ready_min'])
        maint_s=float(l['maintenance_start_min']) if l['maintenance_start_min'] else None
        maint_e=float(l['maintenance_end_min']) if l['maintenance_end_min'] else None
        if start < ready-1e-9: continue
        if maint_s is not None and not (end <= maint_s+1e-9 or start >= maint_e-1e-9):
            continue
        ok=True; prev_end=ready
        for s,e,_ in ladle_intervals[lid]:
            if not (end <= s+1e-9 or start >= e-1e-9): ok=False; break
            if e <= start: prev_end=max(prev_end,e)
        if ok: candidates.append((prev_end,lid))
    if not candidates:
        print('no ladle for',hid,start,end)
        raise SystemExit
    # prefer latest previous end close to start to reduce idle and preheat risk
    candidates.sort(key=lambda x:(abs(start-x[0]), -x[0]))
    prev_end,lid=candidates[0]
    assign[hid]=lid
    ladle_intervals[lid].append((start,end,hid))
    ladle_intervals[lid].sort()
print('ladles used', len({v for v in assign.values()}))
for fam in ['standard','clean','alloy']:
    used=sorted({lid for hid,lid in assign.items() if heats[hid]['ladle_family']==fam})
    print(fam,len(used),used)
# simple overlap check
for lid,ints in ladle_intervals.items():
    for a,b in zip(ints,ints[1:]):
        if a[1] > b[0]+1e-9: print('overlap',lid,a,b)

import csv, json, math
from collections import defaultdict
base='/app/data'
with open(base+'/config.json') as f: cfg=json.load(f)
with open(base+'/heats.csv') as f: heats={r['heat_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(f)}
with open(base+'/casts.csv') as f: casts={r['cast_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(f)}
with open(base+'/route_steps.csv') as f: rs=list(csv.DictReader(f))
with open(base+'/machines.csv') as f: machines=list(csv.DictReader(f))
with open(base+'/transition_rules.csv') as f: tr=list(csv.DictReader(f))
with open(base+'/ladles.csv') as f: ladles={r['ladle_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(f)}
with open(base+'/ladle_grade_compatibility.csv') as f: lgc=list(csv.DictReader(f))
route=defaultdict(list)
for r in rs: route[r['route_id']].append(r)
for k in route: route[k].sort(key=lambda x:int(x['step_no']))
trans={(r['stage'],r['from_family'],r['to_family']):int(r['setup_min']) for r in tr}
compat={(r['ladle_family'],r['grade_family']):int(r['allowed']) for r in lgc}
stage_m=defaultdict(list)
for m in machines: stage_m[m['stage']].append(m)
cast_heats=defaultdict(list)
for h in heats.values(): cast_heats[h['cast_id']].append(h['heat_id'])
for cid in cast_heats: cast_heats[cid].sort(key=lambda hid:int(heats[hid]['cast_sequence']))
ccdur={hid:float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='CC')) for hid in heats}
eligible={}
for hid,h in heats.items():
  for step in route[h['route_id']]:
    st=step['stage']; elig=[]
    for m in stage_m[st]:
      fams=set(m['eligible_families'].split('|'))
      if h['grade_family'] in fams and int(m['min_width_mm'])<=int(h['width_mm'])<=int(m['max_width_mm']): elig.append(m['machine_id'])
    if st=='CC': elig=[h['caster_id']]
    eligible[(hid,st)] = elig

def build(offset_b2=0, offset_a2=0):
  # fixed cast starts with optional offsets
  first={'CAST_A1':210,'CAST_A2':390+offset_a2,'CAST_A3':565,'CAST_A4':760,'CAST_B1':230,'CAST_B2':420+offset_b2,'CAST_B3':615,'CAST_B4':805}
  # adjust sequentially per caster
  cc_start={}; cc_end={}
  for caster,clist in {'CC_1':['CAST_A1','CAST_A2','CAST_A3','CAST_A4'],'CC_2':['CAST_B1','CAST_B2','CAST_B3','CAST_B4']}.items():
    t=max(int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m['CC'] if m['machine_id']==caster)),0)
    prev_last=None; prev_family=None
    for cid in clist:
      fam=casts[cid]['primary_family']; target=first[cid]
      if prev_last is not None: t=max(t, prev_last + trans[('CC', prev_family, fam)])
      t=max(t,target); cur=t
      for hid in cast_heats[cid]:
        cc_start[hid]=cur; cc_end[hid]=cur+ccdur[hid]; cur=cc_end[hid]+int(casts[cid]['target_gap_min'])
      prev_last=cc_end[cast_heats[cid][-1]]; prev_family=fam; t=prev_last
  def sched_stage(stage, jobs, deadlines):
    mach_head={m['machine_id']:float('inf') for m in stage_m[stage]}
    mach_nextfam={m['machine_id']:None for m in stage_m[stage]}
    out={}
    for hid in sorted(jobs,key=lambda hid:deadlines[hid],reverse=True):
      fam=heats[hid]['grade_family']; dur=float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']==stage))
      best=None
      for mid in eligible[(hid,stage)]:
        setup=0 if mach_nextfam[mid] is None else trans[(stage,fam,mach_nextfam[mid])]
        end=min(deadlines[hid]+dur,mach_head[mid]-setup); start=end-dur
        ready=int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m[stage] if m['machine_id']==mid))
        start_min=max(ready,int(heats[hid]['release_min'])) if stage=='BOF' else ready
        if start>=start_min-1e-9 and (best is None or start>best[1]): best=(mid,start,end)
      if best is None: return None,None,None,None
      out[hid]=best; mach_head[best[0]]=best[1]; mach_nextfam[best[0]]=fam
    return out,mach_head,mach_nextfam,0
  rh_jobs=[hid for hid,h in heats.items() if any(s['stage']=='RH' for s in route[h['route_id']])]
  rh_dead={hid:cc_start[hid]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['RH:CC']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='RH')) for hid in rh_jobs}
  rh,_,_,_=sched_stage('RH',rh_jobs,rh_dead)
  if rh is None:return False,None
  lf_jobs=[hid for hid,h in heats.items() if any(s['stage']=='LF' for s in route[h['route_id']])]
  lf_dead={hid:(rh[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:RH']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='LF')) if hid in rh else cc_start[hid]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:CC']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='LF'))) for hid in lf_jobs}
  lf,_,_,_=sched_stage('LF',lf_jobs,lf_dead)
  if lf is None:return False,None
  cas_jobs=[hid for hid,h in heats.items() if any(s['stage']=='CAS' for s in route[h['route_id']])]
  cas_dead={hid:lf[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['CAS:LF']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='CAS')) for hid in cas_jobs}
  cas,_,_,_=sched_stage('CAS',cas_jobs,cas_dead)
  if cas is None:return False,None
  bof_dead={hid:(cas[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['BOF:CAS']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='BOF')) if hid in cas else lf[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['BOF:LF']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='BOF'))) for hid in heats}
  bof,_,_,_=sched_stage('BOF',list(heats),bof_dead)
  if bof is None:return False,None
  # ladles
  usage=sorted((bof[hid][1],cc_end[hid]+int(cfg['ladle_cleanout_min']),hid) for hid in heats)
  ladle_int=defaultdict(list)
  for start,end,hid in usage:
    h=heats[hid]; candidates=[]
    for lid,l in ladles.items():
      if l['ladle_family']"'!=h['"'ladle_family']: continue
      if compat[(l['ladle_family'],h['grade_family'])]"'!=1: continue
      if float(l['"'capacity_tonnes'])+1e-9<float(h['weight_tonnes']): continue
      ready=float(l['ready_min']); ms=float(l['maintenance_start_min']) if l['maintenance_start_min'] else None; me=float(l['maintenance_end_min']) if l['maintenance_end_min'] else None
      if start<ready-1e-9: continue
      if ms is not None and not (end<=ms+1e-9 or start>=me-1e-9): continue
      ok=True; prev=ready
      for s,e,_ in ladle_int[lid]:
        if not (end<=s+1e-9 or start>=e-1e-9): ok=False; break
        if e<=start: prev=max(prev,e)
      if ok: candidates.append((abs(start-prev),-prev,lid))
    if not candidates: return False,('ladle',hid,start,end)
    candidates.sort(); lid=candidates[0][2]; ladle_int[lid].append((start,end,hid)); ladle_int[lid].sort()
  return True,max(cc_end.values())

for oa in [0,20,40,60,80,100]:
  for ob in [0,20,40,60,80,100,120]:
    ok,info=build(ob,oa)
    if ok:
      print('feasible offsets a2,b2',oa,ob,'lastcc',info)
      raise SystemExit
print('none')

# search offsets that yield feasible ladles and horizon <=1260
import csv, json, math
from collections import defaultdict
base='/app/data'
with open(base+'/config.json') as f: cfg=json.load(f)
with open(base+'/heats.csv') as f: heats={r['heat_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(f)}
with open(base+'/casts.csv') as f: casts={r['cast_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(f)}
with open(base+'/route_steps.csv') as f: rs=list(csv.DictReader(f))
with open(base+'/machines.csv') as f: machines=list(csv.DictReader(f))
with open(base+'/transition_rules.csv') as f: tr=list(csv.DictReader(f))
with open(base+'/ladles.csv') as f: ladles={r['ladle_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(f)}
with open(base+'/ladle_grade_compatibility.csv') as f: lgc=list(csv.DictReader(f))
route=defaultdict(list)
for r in rs: route[r['route_id']].append(r)
for k in route: route[k].sort(key=lambda x:int(x['step_no']))
trans={(r['stage'],r['from_family'],r['to_family']):int(r['setup_min']) for r in tr}
compat={(r['ladle_family'],r['grade_family']):int(r['allowed']) for r in lgc}
stage_m=defaultdict(list)
for m in machines: stage_m[m['stage']].append(m)
cast_heats=defaultdict(list)
for h in heats.values(): cast_heats[h['cast_id']].append(h['heat_id'])
for cid in cast_heats: cast_heats[cid].sort(key=lambda hid:int(heats[hid]['cast_sequence']))
ccdur={hid:float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='CC')) for hid in heats}
eligible={}
for hid,h in heats.items():
  for step in route[h['route_id']]:
    st=step['stage']; elig=[]
    for m in stage_m[st]:
      fams=set(m['eligible_families'].split('|'))
      if h['grade_family'] in fams and int(m['min_width_mm'])<=int(h['width_mm'])<=int(m['max_width_mm']): elig.append(m['machine_id'])
    if st=='CC': elig=[h['caster_id']]
    eligible[(hid,st)] = elig

def feas(oa,ob):
  first={'CAST_A1':210,'CAST_A2':390+oa,'CAST_A3':565,'CAST_A4':760,'CAST_B1':230,'CAST_B2':420+ob,'CAST_B3':615,'CAST_B4':805}
  cc_start={}; cc_end={}; first_actual={}
  for caster,clist in {'CC_1':['CAST_A1','CAST_A2','CAST_A3','CAST_A4'],'CC_2':['CAST_B1','CAST_B2','CAST_B3','CAST_B4']}.items():
    t=max(int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m['CC'] if m['machine_id']==caster)),0)
    prev_last=None; prev_family=None
    for cid in clist:
      fam=casts[cid]['primary_family']; target=first[cid]
      if prev_last is not None: t=max(t, prev_last + trans[('CC', prev_family, fam)])
      t=max(t,target); first_actual[cid]=t; cur=t
      for hid in cast_heats[cid]:
        cc_start[hid]=cur; cc_end[hid]=cur+ccdur[hid]; cur=cc_end[hid]+int(casts[cid]['target_gap_min'])
      prev_last=cc_end[cast_heats[cid][-1]]; prev_family=fam; t=prev_last
  last_end=max(cc_end.values())
  if last_end>1260: return None
  def sched_stage(stage, jobs, deadlines):
    mach_head={m['machine_id']:float('inf') for m in stage_m[stage]}
    mach_nextfam={m['machine_id']:None for m in stage_m[stage]}
    out={}
    for hid in sorted(jobs,key=lambda hid:deadlines[hid],reverse=True):
      fam=heats[hid]['grade_family']; dur=float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']==stage))
      best=None
      for mid in eligible[(hid,stage)]:
        setup=0 if mach_nextfam[mid] is None else trans[(stage,fam,mach_nextfam[mid])]
        end=min(deadlines[hid]+dur,mach_head[mid]-setup); start=end-dur
        ready=int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m[stage] if m['machine_id']==mid))
        start_min=max(ready,int(heats[hid]['release_min'])) if stage=='BOF' else ready
        if start>=start_min-1e-9 and (best is None or start>best[1]): best=(mid,start,end)
      if best is None: return None
      out[hid]=best; mach_head[best[0]]=best[1]; mach_nextfam[best[0]]=fam
    return out
  rh_jobs=[hid for hid,h in heats.items() if any(s['stage']=='RH' for s in route[h['route_id']])]
  rh_dead={hid:cc_start[hid]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['RH:CC']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='RH')) for hid in rh_jobs}
  rh=sched_stage('RH',rh_jobs,rh_dead)
  if rh is None:return None
  lf_jobs=[hid for hid,h in heats.items() if any(s['stage']=='LF' for s in route[h['route_id']])]
  lf_dead={hid:(rh[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:RH']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='LF')) if hid in rh else cc_start[hid]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:CC']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='LF'))) for hid in lf_jobs}
  lf=sched_stage('LF',lf_jobs,lf_dead)
  if lf is None:return None
  cas_jobs=[hid for hid,h in heats.items() if any(s['stage']=='CAS' for s in route[h['route_id']])]
  cas_dead={hid:lf[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['CAS:LF']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='CAS')) for hid in cas_jobs}
  cas=sched_stage('CAS',cas_jobs,cas_dead)
  if cas is None:return None
  bof_dead={hid:(cas[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['BOF:CAS']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='BOF')) if hid in cas else lf[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['BOF:LF']-float(next(r['base_duration_min'] for r in route[heats[hid]['route_id']] if r['stage']=='BOF'))) for hid in heats}
  bof=sched_stage('BOF',list(heats),bof_dead)
  if bof is None:return None
  usage=sorted((bof[hid][1],cc_end[hid]+int(cfg['ladle_cleanout_min']),hid) for hid in heats)
  ladle_int=defaultdict(list)
  for start,end,hid in usage:
    h=heats[hid]; candidates=[]
    for lid,l in ladles.items():
      if l['ladle_family']"'!=h['"'ladle_family']: continue
      if compat[(l['ladle_family'],h['grade_family'])]"'!=1: continue
      if float(l['"'capacity_tonnes'])+1e-9<float(h['weight_tonnes']): continue
      ready=float(l['ready_min']); ms=float(l['maintenance_start_min']) if l['maintenance_start_min'] else None; me=float(l['maintenance_end_min']) if l['maintenance_end_min'] else None
      if start<ready-1e-9: continue
      if ms is not None and not (end<=ms+1e-9 or start>=me-1e-9): continue
      ok=True; prev=ready
      for s,e,_ in ladle_int[lid]:
        if not (end<=s+1e-9 or start>=e-1e-9): ok=False; break
        if e<=start: prev=max(prev,e)
      if ok: candidates.append((abs(start-prev),-prev,lid))
    if not candidates: return None
    candidates.sort(); lid=candidates[0][2]; ladle_int[lid].append((start,end,hid)); ladle_int[lid].sort()
  return first_actual,last_end
best=[]
for oa in range(0,181,10):
  for ob in range(0,181,10):
    r=feas(oa,ob)
    if r:
      fa,last=r
      score=oa+ob
      best.append((score,last,oa,ob,fa['CAST_A2'],fa['CAST_B2']))
print('count',len(best))
print(best[:20])

def sched_stage_choose(stage, jobs, deadlines, scen='BASE', fixed_machine=None):
  mach_head={m['machine_id']:float('inf') for m in stage_m[stage]}
  mach_nextfam={m['machine_id']:None for m in stage_m[stage]}
  out={}
  order=sorted(jobs,key=lambda hid:deadlines[hid],reverse=True)
  for hid in order:
    fam=heats[hid]['grade_family']; p=dur(scen,hid,stage)
    mids=[fixed_machine[hid]] if fixed_machine else eligible[(hid,stage)]
    best=None
    for mid in mids:
      setup=0 if mach_nextfam[mid] is None else trans[(stage,fam,mach_nextfam[mid])]
      end=min(deadlines[hid]+p,mach_head[mid]-setup)
      start=end-p
      ready=int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m[stage] if m['machine_id']==mid))
      start_min=max(ready,int(heats[hid]['release_min']) + (delaymap[(scen,hid)] if stage=='BOF' else 0)) if stage=='BOF' else ready
      if start>=start_min-1e-9 and (best is None or start>best[1]):

    if best is None:
      return None
    out[hid]=best; mach_head[best[0]]=best[1]; mach_nextfam[best[0]]=fam
  return out

for start,end,hid in usage:
  h=heats[hid]; candidates=[]
  for lid,l in ladles.items():
    if l['ladle_family']"'!=h['"'ladle_family']: continue
    if compat[(l['ladle_family'],h['grade_family'])]"'!=1: continue
    if float(l['"'capacity_tonnes'])+1e-9<float(h['weight_tonnes']): continue
    ready=float(l['ready_min']); ms=float(l['maintenance_start_min']) if l['maintenance_start_min'] else None; me=float(l['maintenance_end_min']) if l['maintenance_end_min'] else None
    if start<ready-1e-9: continue
    if ms is not None and not (end<=ms+1e-9 or start>=me-1e-9): continue
    ok=True; prev=ready
    for s,e,_ in ladle_int[lid]:

      if e<=start: prev=max(prev,e)
    if ok: candidates.append((abs(start-prev),-prev,lid))
  candidates.sort(); lid=candidates[0][2]; ladle_assign[hid]=lid; ladle_int[lid].append((start,end,hid)); ladle_int[lid].sort()

  if rh2 is None:
    print(scen,'RH infeas'); continue
  lf_dead={hid:(rh2[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:RH']-dur(scen,hid,'LF') if hid in rh2 else cc_start[hid]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:CC']-dur(scen,hid,'LF')) for hid in lf_jobs}

  cas2=sched_stage_choose('CAS',cas_jobs,cas_dead,scen,{hid:base_machine['CAS'][hid] for hid in cas_jobs})
  if cas2 is None:
    print(scen,'CAS infeas'); continue
  bof_dead={hid:(cas2[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['BOF:CAS']-dur(scen,hid,'BOF') if hid in cas2 else lf2[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['BOF:LF']-dur(scen,hid,'BOF')) for hid in heats}
  bof2=sched_stage_choose('BOF',list(heats),bof_dead,scen,{hid:base_machine['BOF'][hid] for hid in heats})
  if bof2 is None:
    print(scen,'BOF infeas'); continue

  ok=True
  by_l=defaultdict(list)
  for hid,lid in ladle_assign.items():
    start=bof2[hid][1]; end=cc_start[hid]+dur(scen,hid,'CC')+int(cfg['ladle_cleanout_min'])
    by_l[lid].append((start,end,hid))

    ints.sort()
    l=ladles[lid]; ms=float(l['maintenance_start_min']) if l['maintenance_start_min'] else None; me=float(l['maintenance_end_min']) if l['maintenance_end_min'] else None
    ready=float(l['ready_min'])
    if ints and ints[0][0] < ready-1e-9: ok=False
    for a,b in zip(ints,ints[1:]):
      if a[1] > b[0]+1e-9: ok=False; print(scen,'ladle overlap',lid,a,b); break
    if ms is not None:
      for it in ints:
        if not (it[1] <= ms+1e-9 or it[0] >= me-1e-9): ok=False; print(scen,'maint conflict',lid,it); break
  print(scen,'ok' if ok else 'ladlebad')

for k in route: route[k].sort(key=lambda x:int(x['step_no']))

        for hid in cast_heats[cid]:
            cc_start[hid]=cur; cc_end[hid]=cur+durbase[(hid,'CC')]; cur=cc_end[hid]

def sched_stage_choose(stage, jobs, deadlines, fixed=None):
    mach_head={m['machine_id']:float('inf') for m in stage_m[stage]}
    mach_nextfam={m['machine_id']:None for m in stage_m[stage]}
    out={}
    for hid in sorted(jobs,key=lambda hid:deadlines[hid],reverse=True):
        fam=heats[hid]['grade_family']; p=durbase[(hid,stage)]
        mids=[fixed[hid]] if fixed else eligible[(hid,stage)]
        best=None
        for mid in mids:
            setup=0 if mach_nextfam[mid] is None else trans[(stage,fam,mach_nextfam[mid])]
            end=min(deadlines[hid]+p,mach_head[mid]-setup); start=end-p
            ready=int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m[stage] if m['machine_id']==mid))
            start_min=max(ready,int(heats[hid]['release_min'])) if stage=='BOF' else ready
            if start>=start_min-1e-9 and (best is None or start>best[1]): best=(mid,start,end)
        if best is None: raise RuntimeError(stage)
        out[hid]=best; mach_head[best[0]]=best[1]; mach_nextfam[best[0]]=fam
    return out

        if start<ready-1e-9: continue

    cand.sort(); lid=cand[0][2]; ladle_assign[hid]=lid; ladle_int[lid].append((start,end,hid)); ladle_int[lid].sort()
# orders
machine_orders=defaultdict(list)
for stage,dct in assign.items():
    if stage=='CC':
        for caster in ['CC_1','CC_2']:
            lst=[hid for hid in heats if heats[hid]['caster_id']==caster]
            lst.sort(key=lambda hid:cc_start[hid])
            machine_orders[(stage,caster)] = lst
    else:
        base_starts={'RH':{hid:rh[hid][1] for hid in rh},'LF':{hid:lf[hid][1] for hid in lf},'CAS':{hid:cas[hid][1] for hid in cas},'BOF':{hid:bof[hid][1] for hid in bof}}[stage]
        for mid in {dct[h] for h in dct}:
            lst=[hid for hid,m in dct.items() if m==mid]
            lst.sort(key=lambda hid:base_starts[hid])
            machine_orders[(stage,mid)] = lst
ladle_orders={}
for lid,ints in ladle_int.items(): ladle_orders[lid]=[hid for _,_,hid in sorted(ints)]

for scen in ['BASE','BOF_SLOW','RH_OUTAGE','OXYGEN_TIGHT']:
    model=Model()
    model.setParam('display/verblevel',0)
    t={}
    tard={}
    tempv={}
    for hid in heats:
        for st in all_steps[hid]:
            t[(hid,st)] = model.addVar(vtype='I', lb=0, name=f't[{hid},{st}]')
        tard[hid]=model.addVar(lb=0, name=f'tard[{hid}]')
        tempv[hid]=model.addVar(lb=0, name=f'temp[{hid}]')
    gapv={}
    # release and machine ready
    for hid in heats:
        model.addCons(t[(hid,'BOF')] >= int(heats[hid]['release_min']) + delaymap[(scen,hid)])
        for st in all_steps[hid]:
            mid=assign[st][hid]
            ready=int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m[st] if m['machine_id']==mid))
            model.addCons(t[(hid,st)] >= ready)
    # route precedence
    for hid in heats:
        steps=all_steps[hid]
        for a,b in zip(steps, steps[1:]):
            trkey=f'{a}:{b}'
            model.addCons(t[(hid,b)] >= t[(hid,a)] + durbase[(hid,a)]*multmap[(scen,a)][0] + cfg['min_transfer_min'][trkey])
    # machine orders
    for (stage,mid),lst in machine_orders.items():
        for i,j in zip(lst,lst[1:]):
            setup=trans[(stage,heats[i]['grade_family'],heats[j]['grade_family'])]
            model.addCons(t[(j,stage)] >= t[(i,stage)] + durbase[(i,stage)]*multmap[(scen,stage)][0] + setup)
    # cast continuity within strings zero gap and same caster fixed order already through machine order but reinforce with string seq
    for cid,hids in cast_heats.items():
        for i,j in zip(hids,hids[1:]):
            gapv[(i,j)] = model.addVar(lb=0, name=f'gap[{i},{j}]')
            model.addCons(t[(j,'CC')] >= t[(i,'CC')] + durbase[(i,'CC')])
            model.addCons(gapv[(i,j)] >= t[(j,'CC')] - (t[(i,'CC')] + durbase[(i,'CC')] + int(casts[cid]['target_gap_min'])))
            model.addCons(t[(j,'CC')] <= t[(i,'CC')] + durbase[(i,'CC')] + int(casts[cid]['max_gap_min']))
    # published first-string lower bounds
    for cid,hids in cast_heats.items():
        model.addCons(t[(hids[0],'CC')] >= pub[cid])
    # ladle orders
    for lid,lst in ladle_orders.items():
        for i,j in zip(lst,lst[1:]):
            model.addCons(t[(j,'BOF')] >= t[(i,'CC')] + durbase[(i,'CC')] + int(cfg['ladle_cleanout_min']))
    # maintenance simple: each assigned heat interval must avoid maintenance if obviously after previous or before next. use conservative lower bound if overlap possible
    for hid,lid in ladle_assign.items():
        l=ladles[lid]
        if l['maintenance_start_min']:
            ms=float(l['maintenance_start_min']); me=float(l['maintenance_end_min'])
            # if base BOF after maintenance, keep it after; else if base end before maintenance, keep it before using upper bound on CC.
            base_b=bof[hid][1]; base_e=cc_end[hid]+int(cfg['ladle_cleanout_min'])
            if base_b >= me:
                model.addCons(t[(hid,'BOF')] >= me)
            elif base_e <= ms:
                model.addCons(t[(hid,'CC')] + durbase[(hid,'CC')] + int(cfg['ladle_cleanout_min']) <= ms)
    # tardiness and temperature
    for hid in heats:
        cc_end_expr = t[(hid,'CC')] + durbase[(hid,'CC')]
        model.addCons(tard[hid] >= cc_end_expr - int(heats[hid]['due_min']))
        final_stage='RH' if 'RH' in all_steps[hid] else 'LF'
        final_dur=durbase[(hid,final_stage)]*multmap[(scen,final_stage)][0]
        mintr=cfg['min_transfer_min'][f'{final_stage}:CC']
        wait_expr=t[(hid,'CC')] - (t[(hid,final_stage)] + final_dur + mintr)
        allowed=float(heats[hid]['min_superheat_c'])/float(heats[hid]['temp_drop_c_per_min'])
        model.addCons(tempv[hid] >= float(heats[hid]['temp_drop_c_per_min'])*(wait_expr - allowed))
        # hard max wait to cast from end of final refining, no transfer subtraction
        model.addCons(t[(hid,'CC')] - (t[(hid,final_stage)] + final_dur) <= cfg['max_wait_to_cast_min'])
    obj = quicksum(int(heats[hid]['priority'])*cfg['tardiness_penalty_per_priority_min']*tard[hid] + cfg['temperature_loss_penalty_per_c']*tempv[hid] for hid in heats)
    obj += quicksum(cfg['caster_break_penalty_per_min']*gapv[k] for k in gapv)
    # small holding penalty to keep BOF and intermediate stages late
    for hid in heats:
        obj += 0.05*(t[(hid,'CC')] - t[(hid,'BOF')])
    model.setObjective(obj,'minimize')
    model.optimize()
    st=model.getStatus()
    print('scenario',scen,'status',st,'obj',model.getObjVal())
    last=max(model.getVal(t[(hid,'CC')])+durbase[(hid,'CC')] for hid in heats)
    print(' last cc',last)
    # quick ladle check
    bad=0
    by_l=defaultdict(list)
    for hid,lid in ladle_assign.items():
        by_l[lid].append((model.getVal(t[(hid,'BOF')]), model.getVal(t[(hid,'CC')])+durbase[(hid,'CC')]+int(cfg['ladle_cleanout_min']), hid))
    for lid,ints in by_l.items():
        ints.sort()
        for a,b in zip(ints,ints[1:]):
            if a[1] > b[0] + 1e-6:
                bad+=1
                break
    print(' ladle bad count',bad)

def try_pub(a1,b1,b2):
  first={'CAST_A1':a1,'CAST_A2':0,'CAST_A3':0,'CAST_A4':0,'CAST_B1':b1,'CAST_B2':b2,'CAST_B3':0,'CAST_B4':0}
  # zero gaps, later casts earliest after prev unless b2 anchor forces later
  cc_start={}; cc_end={}; actual={}

            model.addCons(t[(hid,st)] >= ready)
    for hid in heats:

            model.addCons(t[(hid,b)] >= t[(hid,a)] + durbase[(hid,a)]*multmap[(scen,a)][0] + cfg['min_transfer_min'][f'{a}:{b}'])
    for (stage,mid),lst in machine_orders.items():
        for i,j in zip(lst,lst[1:]):
            model.addCons(t[(j,stage)] >= t[(i,stage)] + durbase[(i,stage)]*multmap[(scen,stage)][0] + trans[(stage,heats[i]['grade_family'],heats[j]['grade_family'])])

            model.addCons(t[(j,'CC')] <= t[(i,'CC')] + durbase[(i,'CC')] + int(casts[cid]['max_gap_min']))

            elif base_e <= ms: model.addCons(t[(hid,'CC')] + durbase[(hid,'CC')] + int(cfg['ladle_cleanout_min']) <= ms)

        model.addCons(tard[hid] >= cc_end_expr - int(heats[hid]['due_min']))

        allowed=float(heats[hid]['min_superheat_c'])/float(heats[hid]['temp_drop_c_per_min'])
        model.addCons(tempv[hid] >= float(heats[hid]['temp_drop_c_per_min'])*(wait_expr - allowed))
        model.addCons(t[(hid,'CC')] - (t[(hid,final)] + f_dur) <= cfg['max_wait_to_cast_min'])
    obj=quicksum(int(heats[hid]['priority'])*cfg['tardiness_penalty_per_priority_min']*tard[hid] + cfg['temperature_loss_penalty_per_c']*tempv[hid] for hid in heats)
    obj += quicksum(cfg['caster_break_penalty_per_min']*gapv[k] for k in gapv)
    obj += quicksum(0.05*(t[(hid,'CC')] - t[(hid,'BOF')]) for hid in heats)
    model.setObjective(obj,'minimize'); model.optimize()
    print(scen,model.getStatus(),'last',max(model.getVal(t[(hid,'CC')])+durbase[(hid,'CC')] for hid in heats),'obj',model.getObjVal())

all_steps={hid:[s['stage'] for s in route[h['route_id']]] for hid,h in heats.items()}
durbase={(hid,s['stage']):float(s['base_duration_min']) for hid,h in heats.items() for s in route[h['route_id']]}
pub={'CAST_A1':160,'CAST_A2':0,'CAST_A3':0,'CAST_A4':0,'CAST_B1':170,'CAST_B2':540,'CAST_B3':0,'CAST_B4':0}
# base heuristic as before
cc_start={}; cc_end={}; actual_first={}
for caster,clist in {'CC_1':['CAST_A1','CAST_A2','CAST_A3','CAST_A4'],'CC_2':['CAST_B1','CAST_B2','CAST_B3','CAST_B4']}.items():
    t=max(int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m['CC'] if m['machine_id']==caster)),0)
    prev_last=None; prev_family=None
    for cid in clist:
        fam=casts[cid]['primary_family']; target=pub[cid]
        if prev_last is not None: t=max(t, prev_last + trans[('CC',prev_family,fam)])
        t=max(t,target); actual_first[cid]=t; cur=t
        for hid in cast_heats[cid]:
            cc_start[hid]=cur; cc_end[hid]=cur+durbase[(hid,'CC')]; cur=cc_end[hid]
        prev_last=cc_end[cast_heats[cid][-1]]; prev_family=fam; t=prev_last
eligible={}
for hid,h in heats.items():
    for stage in all_steps[hid]:
        elig=[]
        for m in stage_m[stage]:
            fams=set(m['eligible_families'].split('|'))
            if h['grade_family'] in fams and int(m['min_width_mm'])<=int(h['width_mm'])<=int(m['max_width_mm']): elig.append(m['machine_id'])
        if stage=='CC': elig=[h['caster_id']]
        eligible[(hid,stage)] = elig

def sched_stage_choose(stage, jobs, deadlines, fixed=None):
    mach_head={m['machine_id']:float('inf') for m in stage_m[stage]}
    mach_nextfam={m['machine_id']:None for m in stage_m[stage]}
    out={}
    for hid in sorted(jobs,key=lambda hid:deadlines[hid],reverse=True):
        fam=heats[hid]['grade_family']; p=durbase[(hid,stage)]
        mids=[fixed[hid]] if fixed else eligible[(hid,stage)]
        best=None
        for mid in mids:
            setup=0 if mach_nextfam[mid] is None else trans[(stage,fam,mach_nextfam[mid])]
            end=min(deadlines[hid]+p,mach_head[mid]-setup); start=end-p
            ready=int(next(m['ready_min'] for m in stage_m[stage] if m['machine_id']==mid))
            start_min=max(ready,int(heats[hid]['release_min'])) if stage=='BOF' else ready
            if start>=start_min-1e-9 and (best is None or start>best[1]): best=(mid,start,end)
        if best is None: raise RuntimeError(stage)
        out[hid]=best; mach_head[best[0]]=best[1]; mach_nextfam[best[0]]=fam
    return out
rh_jobs=[hid for hid in heats if 'RH' in all_steps[hid]]
rh_dead={hid:cc_start[hid]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['RH:CC']-durbase[(hid,'RH')] for hid in rh_jobs}
rh=sched_stage_choose('RH',rh_jobs,rh_dead)
lf_jobs=[hid for hid in heats if 'LF' in all_steps[hid]]
lf_dead={hid:(rh[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:RH']-durbase[(hid,'LF')] if hid in rh else cc_start[hid]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['LF:CC']-durbase[(hid,'LF')]) for hid in lf_jobs}
lf=sched_stage_choose('LF',lf_jobs,lf_dead)
cas_jobs=[hid for hid in heats if 'CAS' in all_steps[hid]]
cas_dead={hid:lf[hid][1]-cfg['min_transfer_min']['CAS:LF']-durbase[(hid,'CAS')] for hid in cas_jobs}
